# Candidate Access fixtures

## Original column-LVAL map growth INSERT inputs

Original project helper
[LongValueMapGrowthFixture.java](ace16_insert_long_value_map_growth/LongValueMapGrowthFixture.java)
uses the same reviewed Jackcess 5.0.4 JAR and bounded channel as the external
Memo/OLE corpus below. It establishes real early selected ownership, then writes
an independent 430/700-row Padding table through public builders/DML. All payloads
are generated, no upstream fixture/code is copied, and no source byte/map setter,
padding search or selector change forces the observed transition. Expressions
remain disabled and linked opens refuse. Originals stay private under
`.cache/ace16-insert-long-value-map-growth-source-first-20261028/`.

SOURCE17 capture and eight separate raw/catalog gates pass before construction.
The [maintained proof](ace16_insert_long_value_map_growth/README.md) derives sixteen
whole images before a fresh-target committed producer build, then verifies
sixteen native candidates and sixteen fresh combined reader/map calls. A separate
public32 phase also passes sixteen transitions without supplying golden bytes.
Both layouts/Memo modes qualify actual owned-row rebase and promotion with chain
links around new owned bitmap metadata. The
[compact result](results/ace16-insert-long-value-map-growth-2026-10-28.json)
pins all eight original identities and phase receipts. Old orphan chunks and
all Padding/Sentinel rows remain preserved. Authentic next-block missing-pointer,
missing-map initialization and native Access/Windows saves remain separate gaps;
no such result is inferred from these small originals.

## Original external Memo/OLE INSERT inputs

Original project helper
[LongValueInsertFixture.java](ace16_insert_external_long_values/LongValueInsertFixture.java)
creates four inert originals with the reviewed Jackcess 5.0.4 JAR
(SHA-256 `19a7494a56f439a57fda85c059b019b665802085cec5ae8186d1e028cc1bdfbc`).
It reuses the existing bounded file-channel helper. No upstream code or database
fixture is copied. Public row creation/deletion is explicit, expression evaluation
stays disabled, and linked database opens are refused. Originals remain private
under `.cache/ace16-insert-external-long-value-source-first-20261028/`.

| Original | Bytes | SHA-256 |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| V2016 raw Memo | 446,464 | `9a6b7e0e5b0984e51e1af3170ff92e641b48809db2f74c17ab634f80993c0359` |
| V2016 compressed Memo | 446,464 | `1f5c45d48680ef13c474ebe9b6a6a3e4a745ac7dcdca94c5f3b2b96d82d11c04` |
| V2019 raw Memo | 520,192 | `986ddd0db110ef562ead874c3c27dbba514a6d2154cb49bf0836d17f3966f125` |
| V2019 compressed Memo | 520,192 | `e8c39e4b9e52f4204e5e58338d2180efaba980e193c4be078fc84aaf771454de` |

Each original has six live parents, ordinary OLE and Memo columns, two physical
I32 indexes with true aliases, and an untouched Sentinel. The saved catalog-wide
gate observes one preserved orphan chunk per long column after public deletion;
no source patch or orphan predicate forces that result. SOURCE9 capture passed
separately from saved-only qualification. The
[maintained proof](ace16_insert_external_long_values/README.md) derives sixteen
complete request images before producer execution, then verifies sixteen native
candidates and sixteen fresh combined getter/map calls. The independent public
phase retains its initial two successful children, footer-proof correction and
thirty unique remaining children. The shared-target stale build and corrective
fresh-target compilation are separately recorded. Native Access/Windows saves
remain unrun; this corpus does not qualify map-growth or initialization cases.

## Native index collection experiment recipes

Original MIT [IndexColumnsFixture.java](IndexColumnsFixture.java), SHA-256
`2a4c96894698e1cec1acb3dfed21e650b95e1502b52745800952a84d36f45947`,
creates fresh inert ACE 16 and Jet 4 inputs, each with six tables covering
one/two/ten-column indexes and one/twenty-four logical indexes. It uses the
existing reviewed Jackcess 5.0.4 JAR and copies no database, third-party code or
active application content. Generated databases and isolated build copies remain
ignored; the recipe refuses existing fixture paths.

The existing IndexOracle getter supplies independent names, flags, native/physical
identifiers, roots and key ordering. The new
[verifier](verify_index_column_variants.py) compares these against all
three isolated container variants and checks unchanged inputs/binaries. Its
capacity/spill-derived allocation counts concern collection constructors only.
See the [experiment procedure](INDEX_COLUMN_COLLECTIONS.md); neither the
recipe nor independent getter agreement establishes native Access compatibility.

| Original input | Bytes | SHA-256 |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| index-columns-V2016.accdb | 602,112 | `ad2b539848fbe8d2a6952cbc285be4b7f946a1798dd184d4b551785bac7db4ec` |
| index-columns-V2000.mdb | 331,776 | `1392c2f51ce954e7cfc8551f8e8f0a66dadbc922865016661ad537f226a23994` |

The two runs retain all 675 timing samples and pass 2,094 independent metadata/
constructor controls, with unchanged binary/input hashes. Source/recipe/JAR hashes
in the sidecars are explicitly observations made after timing, separately from
the before/after input and executable checks. Normalization now supports either
qualified production container in isolated copies, keeping the Vec counterfactual
reproducible after adoption. Original inputs stay ignored and unchanged.

## Full-width Decimal arithmetic controls

The original [Python runner](compare_decimal_boundaries.py), SHA-256
`77d6f5473024914c99cff35c3f1ca8a7e41bcfab84fe445e3b07d9281e284ad6`,
uses only exact Fraction arithmetic and independently stated 96-bit/scale bounds.
It copies no third-party code or fixture, opens no database and executes only
explicit source-free TableCraft queries. The [recorded report](results/decimal-boundaries-2026-10-24.json)
retains the deterministic seed, binary/source hashes and all 23,460 passing control
counts. The binary hash is checked before and after execution. Numeric equality,
subtypes and empty-stdout failures are checked separately; representational scale,
full Variant promotion and native Access execution are not claimed. See
[boundary evidence](DECIMAL_ARITHMETIC.md#full-width-rational-boundary-checks).

## Larger decoded attachment inputs

Original MIT [LargeDecodedAttachmentFixture.java](LargeDecodedAttachmentFixture.java)
creates inert native data using the reviewed Jackcess 5.0.4 JAR, SHA-256
`19a7494a56f439a57fda85c059b019b665802085cec5ae8186d1e028cc1bdfbc`.
Each file has a three-parent table: one 3 MiB repeated-byte compressed file,
one deterministic 1,500,000-byte stored file, and an empty parent. Actual getters
confirm codec tags 1/0 and encoded lengths below the existing 2 MiB ceiling.
The recipe refuses existing paths and adds no VBA, macros, SQL or active content.
Its read-only inspection mode disables expression evaluation and reports file
lengths, SHA-256 digests, encoding tags and encoded lengths. No third-party
database or implementation is copied.

## Retained String repetition controls

The original [runner](compare_raw_repetition.py) passes 2,432 controls.
Python integer-list repetition supplies exact unit expectations for authored
boundaries, seeded units, complete source tails and zero/positive counts across
both CLI evaluators. Binary observations, rounded regional counts, NULL gates,
odd/empty refusals, budget failures and strict final publication
remain separate from successful raw observations.

Four registered native-policy inputs supply IDs and character fields through inert
TableDataOracle getters. TableCraft evaluates sequential/selected primary-index
queries under stored and explicit General choices. Before/after hashes confirm
unchanged originals; reviewed JAR identity is checked and no third-party artifact
is added. Existing repetition-state controls pass 220 checks over registered
20,000-row indexed inputs; the rounded repetition/search runner passes 1,200
controls with registered ScalarSamples. These are independent engineering checks,
not observed Microsoft Access unit/coercion behavior.


## Retained String trimming controls

The original [runner](compare_raw_trimming.py) passes 3,786 controls.
Independent Python integer-list prefix/suffix removal checks exact unit results
and lengths across both CLI evaluators, including isolated units, surrogate
pairs, NUL, tabs, nonbreaking spaces, empty/all-space and unaligned ranges.
Invalid raw lengths, eager failures and strict final publication are checked
separately from successful observations.

Native-source controls reuse the four registered native-policy inputs through
inert TableDataOracle getters. TableCraft executes sequential/selected primary-
index length queries under stored and explicit General choices; all before/after
hashes remain unchanged. The reviewed JAR identity is checked and no new
third-party artifact is added. Existing scalar trimming/casing and retained-cell
recipes pass 336 and 1,053 controls. These are independent engineering checks;
native Access edge/coercion qualification remains pending.


## Retained String replacement controls

The original [runner](compare_raw_replacement.py) and
[General helper](RawGeneralReplacementOracle.java) pass 9,068 controls.
Python supplies independent nonoverlapping binary unit replacement; General
cases use the reviewed Jackcess encoder through the existing original short-span
model. UTF-16 hex preserves isolated units. Length and binary StrComp observations
check exact result units, including expansions, ignored prefixes, suffixes inside
pairs, deletion, count limits and repairing final pairs.

Native-source controls reuse four registered native-policy inputs through inert
TableDataOracle getters, then TableCraft evaluates queries under database/global
General policies with sequential and selected primary-index reads. Before/after
hashes confirm unchanged originals. Artifact identity is checked; helpers compile
temporarily and no third-party artifact is added. The original count-conversion
runner also passes 798 controls over the separate registered ScalarSamples inputs;
the scalar runner passes 324 source-free controls. These do not establish native
Access SQL or locale behavior. The new runner uses smaller projection batches to
retain existing default step limits.


## Raw General substring-search controls

The original [runner](compare_raw_general_search.py) and
[raw oracle helper](RawGeneralSearchOracle.java) pass 4,544 controls.
The helper reuses the existing original GeneralSearchOracle span model and
reviewed Jackcess key encoder; all synthetic inputs fit the complete short-key
profile. UTF-16 hex output retains isolated units without replacement encoding.
Cases include both General families, valid/isolated surrogates, ignored keys,
expansions, accents, spaces and forward/reverse bounds.

Four registered native-policy inputs additionally supply source rows through
inert TableDataOracle getters, followed by TableCraft queries under database and
explicit General policies with sequential and selected primary-index reads.
Before/after hashes confirm unchanged files. JAR identity is checked, compiled
helpers are temporary and no third-party artifact is added. Decoded General and
binary search recipes still pass 268 and 3,634 controls. These checks establish
the provisional span model; native Access search/locale qualification is pending.


## Retained binary String search controls

The original [runner](compare_raw_string_search.py) passes 3,634 controls
against independent Python integer-list window positions, including isolated
UTF-16 units, overlapping patterns, default reverse starts, empty/NULL inputs,
unaligned ranges and refusals. Both CLI evaluators run the source-free controls.
Native-source controls reuse the four registered native-policy inputs through
sequential and selected primary-index reads with explicit policy choices.
The reviewed Jackcess artifact and inert TableDataOracle supply rows only;
TableCraft executes SQL. All four before/after hashes remain unchanged.
No third-party artifact was added. Existing General search/replacement and raw
database StrComp runners pass 268 and 9,255 controls. These are independent
engineering checks, not observed Windows Access search behavior.


## Raw database-mode StrComp controls

The original [runner](compare_raw_database_comparison.py) passes 9,255
controls against the registered short General key vectors and existing four
native-policy inputs. It covers both model families, modes 2/omitted/-1, raw and
decoded inputs, NULL/refusal controls and sequential/selected primary-index reads.
The reviewed Jackcess artifact and inert TableDataOracle supply IDs only;
TableCraft executes SQL. No third-party artifact is added by this recipe.
Before/after SHA-256 checks confirm all four native inputs remain unchanged.
The existing binary StrComp and extrema runners still pass 1,102 and 791
controls respectively. This establishes the current explicit models, not Windows
Access function-specific padding/locale compatibility.


## Retained String extrema controls

The original [extrema runner](compare_raw_string_extrema.py) passes 791
controls. Python selects first equal representatives from all reviewed short
General vectors in original, reversed and seeded orders, with NULL and grouped
inputs. Lengths and binary StrComp observations verify selected byte contents.
These 215 source-free controls are separate from 576 native-source controls.

The reviewed Jackcess JAR and original TableDataOracle getters supply IDs 1..6
from each registered policy input. Python supplies independent ASCII/selection
expectations; TableCraft alone evaluates aggregate/domain SQL. Native runs cover
database/default General policies, both explicit family overrides, sequential and
selected primary-index reads, bounded criteria and empty populations. All four
input SHA-256 values remain unchanged. No new third-party fixture or dependency
was added. Native Access qualification and benchmarks were not run.

## Retained String key controls

The original [key runner](compare_raw_string_keys.py) passes 406 controls
using all reviewed short General vectors from the order runner and one NULL row
per family. Python groups/sorts the recorded keys and supplies stable ties,
representative lengths, distinct counts and selected-record expectations. Cases
cover singleton Strings/Error data, odd-unit comparison refusal, eager failures
and strict final publication. Balanced String constructors and bounded projection
batches retain all vectors within existing parser/binder limits; no limit was
raised. Only TableCraft executes SQL. No new third-party fixture was copied.

The order, predicate, LIKE, retained-cell and native-policy recipes still pass
17,345/7,113/2,745/1,053/1,844 controls. All four registered native-policy input
hashes remain unchanged. These are provisional model/source controls; native
Windows Access qualification and benchmarks were not run.

## Raw String order controls

The original [raw String order runner](compare_raw_string_order.py) passes
17,345 controls against the existing reviewed short General key vectors in
`crates/engines/tablecraft-ace/src/collation/key/tests/vectors.tsv`. It pairs both
families' vectors of at most four units through all six SQL operators, including
isolated surrogates, and adds list/range/subquery/refusal cases. These native-index
vectors qualify the explicit provisional full-string model, not Microsoft Access
SQL execution. No new third-party fixture was copied or downloaded. Existing
raw LIKE/predicate and retained-cell runners pass 2,745/7,113/1,053 controls; the
native-policy runner passes 1,844 controls over unchanged registered hashes.
Native Access qualification and benchmarks remain unrun.

## Raw UTF-16 LIKE controls

The original [raw LIKE runner](compare_raw_like.py) passes 2,745 controls
under both General families and ANSI modes. Python supplies exact byte slicing,
unit wildcard counts, NULL truth and explicit refusal expectations over fixed
and seeded units, including isolated surrogates. Additional cases preserve the
existing provisional General expansion profile. Pure generated unit-glob tests
use an independent exhaustive matcher. No fixture/dependency was downloaded,
copied or generated; only TableCraft evaluates SQL. Native Access qualification
and benchmarks remain unrun. The existing NULL/category predicate recipe still
passes 7,113 controls.

The original [predicate runner](compare_raw_string_predicates.py) passes
7,113 source-free controls. Python supplies Unknown truth for String/NULL pairs,
empty quantifier outcomes and the issued-Variant type-category rule over fixed
and seeded raw producers. Both CLI modes cover scalar comparisons/lists/ranges;
bound runs include retained derived cells, subqueries and explicit declaration
flags. Explicit ANSI-89/92 runs also cover LIKE/NOT LIKE NULL truth through direct
and retained cells, missing-mode refusal and eager errors.
Eager/state/publication refusals remain checked. Existing retained-cell
and native-policy recipes pass 1,053 and 1,844 controls over unchanged registered
native-policy inputs. No third-party artifact/dependency was added. Native Windows
Access and benchmarks were not run.

## Independent lossless concatenation controls

The expanded original [concatenation recipe](compare_concatenation.py)
passes 2,918 source-free controls through eval and run. Python constructs complete

decodes only final valid Text. Seeded pairs cover paired/unpaired surrogates,
control characters, NULL, Empty-Text query expressions, explicit numeric policy
and eager failures. Both ampersand and String plus share the unit-pair model;
plus additionally checks NULL propagation, mixed numeric values/subtypes and

Access and benchmarks were not run; no third-party artifact or dependency was added.

## Independent raw UTF-16 observations

The expanded original [byte recipe](compare_byte_strings.py) checks 35,336
source-free values/refusals against Python UTF-16LE byte models in both query
commands. Added cases cover Len/AscW over complete raw units and ChrW inputs for
every code unit D800–DFFF, plus signed spellings, byte lengths and String subtype
inspection. Ordinary Left/Right/Mid ranges now also cover omitted lengths,
surrogate halves and maximum Long counts using Python byte ranges in multiples
of two. Direct outer slices also select empty ranges from those intermediates
before scalar publication. Direct surrogate publication stays refused. No binary, third-party
source or additional dependency was added. Native fixture replays and Windows
Access were not run for this batch.

retaining strict final publication and odd-byte Unicode observation refusals.
The existing standalone CStr and concatenation recipes also pass 3,224 and 1,542
source-free controls respectively; their optional native inputs were not supplied.

ordered admission checks after removal of the former 28-place ceiling. Native
Microsoft Access and native fixture replays were not run for this batch.

query comparisons using the same independent cursor expectations and unchanged hashes.
All new code is original repository MIT test/host material; no third-party source
or database binary is added. [Selection scope](../access/indexes/NATIVE_INDEXES.md#exact-guid-point-selections)
keeps partial audits, SQL ordering, writes and native Access qualification separate.

## Projected DISTINCT order controls

Original `testing/compare_projected_sort.py` checks 144 deterministic source-free
SELECT/UNION inputs using independent Python sets, counters and NULL ordering.

provider refusal, one-draw explicit random aliases and per-field text policies.
See [ordering scope](../query/QUERY_ORDERING.md); these are independent current-profile
controls, not native Access execution or optimizer evidence.

fields from the existing ScalarSamples V2000/V2007/V2016/V2019 files. Reviewed

a positive control. See [scope](../expressions/formatting/FORMAT.md#interleaved-numeric-literals).

## General UTF-16 LIKE pattern controls

The original `GeneralLikeOracle.java`/`compare_general_like.py` recipe now supplies

Apache-2.0 source header in a temporary directory and replaces only the index
string-preparation adapter to retain pattern spaces and avoid index truncation.
The unchanged independent encoder supplies keys and all raw single-unit forms;
an original recursive matcher and explicit finite pattern models supply truth.
The patched source SHA-256 is recorded in [NATIVE_TEXT.md](../access/indexes/NATIVE_TEXT.md#general-utf-16-like-matching).
No external source/class/database is tracked and original hashes remain unchanged.
This qualifies the explicit UTF-16/weighted model, including excluded-prefix
negation, not native Access LIKE syntax/semantics or error ordering.

## Derived General LIKE unit dictionaries

The [derived LIKE unit sets](../../crates/engines/tablecraft-ace/data/general/README.md)
add no database fixture. They are generated by the existing General weight model
for every raw UTF-16 unit, preserving pattern-literal spaces and widened positions.
Original regeneration tooling reproduces both serialized sets byte-for-byte.
Normal tests verify CRC/SHA-256, all unit memberships and exact distinct-key
counts, so extra dictionary keys cannot hide behind coverage checks. Existing
Apache-2.0 weight-data provenance/notices apply. These checks establish the
derived set, not native Access LIKE behavior or an implemented query matcher.

The separately derived French legacy LIKE dictionary adds no external fixture
or copied code. The original ignored `emit_french_like_dictionary` test encodes
all raw UTF-16 units with the existing full French encoder, retaining pattern

[French LIKE contract](../access/indexes/FRENCH_LIKE.md) separates the engineering
matcher and independent pattern checks from native Access qualification.

## General text-key reference corpus

`GeneralKeysOracle.java` is an original reference-driver recipe over the reviewed
Jackcess 5.0.4 encoder. The checked-in key corpus contains 280 UTF-16 string
vectors with both index directions (560 expected keys) and 512 block SHA-256
fingerprints covering every UTF-16 unit, two families and both directions
(262,144 keys). Inputs include individual surrogate units and long prefixes, so
they are represented as hex UTF-16 units, not repaired UTF-8 strings. These are
deterministic generated vectors, not third-party database bytes.

The separate bundled weight data and Rust adaptation retain Apache-2.0 license
and copyright notices; [provenance and hashes](../../crates/engines/tablecraft-ace/data/general/README.md)
record the reviewed source/binary artifacts and normalization recipe. Exact
reference encoding agrees with the Rust implementation; the weight source is
shared, so this does not independently prove native Access weight behavior.
No database application content is executed. Regeneration requires the exact
reviewed binary JAR; ordinary tests/builds use only tracked artifacts.

The original `SortOrderOracle.java` driver and `compare_sort_orders.py` use the
existing read-only Java comparison closure to check root and text-column
LCID/variant/version. Ten already registered or original generated inputs agree;
the exact selection and limits are in [NATIVE_TEXT.md](../access/indexes/NATIVE_TEXT.md). Source
hashes stay unchanged. Original Rust header goldens also cover zero/unknown
descriptors without adopting the reference reader's fallback. No new third-party
database or weight-table bytes are introduced; native comparison/LIKE/index
semantics are not qualified by these metadata tests.

## Distribution filename regressions

The backend's `tests/support/deployment.rs` constructs original independent CAB
1.3 geometry and uses existing flate2 solely for MSZIP blocks. Backend/CLI tests
wrap the registered MIT example and original Agile derivatives in temporary
uncompressed/MSZIP containers, preserving their exact database bytes. Reservation
and placeholder overlay tests do not contain or claim a valid digital signature.
No third-party package fixture or source is copied; signed Access-produced and
LZX deployment fixtures remain unqualified.

`apps/tablecraft-cli/tests/distribution_names.rs` creates temporary same-byte
copies of the registered MIT Access example under `.accdr`/`.accde`/`.ACCDE`
names and the repository-authored Jet 4 golden image under `.mde`. It compares
row output with SELECT and checks tables/counts, inspection, invalid-content
refusal and unchanged bytes. No original is renamed or modified. These prove
filename-independent routing, not Access compilation, runtime behavior, template
reading or signed-package validity. No compiled/template/package third-party
fixture is introduced or qualified by this test.

`LegacyEncryptionVectors.java OUTPUT_DIRECTORY` optionally creates an original

With `--check-add`, the same comparison additionally adds protection to fresh
plaintext derivatives (or the original unencoded password-set Jet sources under
their existing credentials). It compares independent rows and decoded SID digests,
then uses the original `RemoveAddedEncryptionOracle.java` driver to independently
remove protection from fresh candidates and compare the complete recovered image.

cover codes 1/2/3/5/6 with fresh-key checks and exact byte recovery except the
documented ACE 2007 Agile header upgrade. No extra fixture bytes are tracked.

The [envelope comparison](../access/storage/NATIVE_ENCRYPTION.md#references-and-evidence) checks
version/family/length/flags, continued encoded-read refusal and unchanged input
hashes. These are reference-inspection controls, not decrypted-row comparisons.
The additional legacy comparison now checks all 28 Jet 4 encoded pages, all
105 CryptoAPI RC4 pages and all 107 compatibility AES pages, plus exact native
rows/queries/counts and unchanged input hashes. `LegacyEncryptionVectors.java`
is an original JCE/BC recipe for public fixed crypto inputs; its checked-in
`legacy/vectors.txt` is generated output owned by this repository, not third-party
database bytes. No upstream source/fixtures were copied.
Their creation recipes, Access build, Windows observations and ACE 16 layout
qualification are not established by the file names. Mutation tests use separate
synthetic memory images; the upstream inputs remain untouched.

The separate [Agile page comparison](../access/storage/NATIVE_ENCRYPTION.md#working-agile-codec)
now checks all 104 data pages in `db2007-enc.accdb` and 94 in `db2013-enc.accdb`
against read-only Jackcess Encrypt output, using their public upstream test
passwords through stdin. Original hashes remain unchanged. These raw-page checks
do not qualify encrypted table queries, rewritten outputs or native Windows use.
`AgileCryptoVectors.java` is an original deterministic JCE recipe for public
Unicode/mixed-parameter constants in Rust tests; it creates no database file.
Primitive AES-CBC vectors are from NIST SP 800-38A F.2 (US federal publication).

`compare_encrypted_browsing.py` now checks the original Agile files through normal
CLI table inventory/schema/row/query operations against independent encrypted
cursors: one and seven exact rows, including NULL/text. Credential failures,
all-table output and source hashes are checked. The CI helper
`crates/engines/tablecraft-ace/tests/support/agile.rs` independently writes original
deterministic AES-128/SHA-256 derivatives of the registered MIT example in memory
or fresh temporary paths. It uses a public Unicode test password and fixed salts/
keys, never production encryption defaults. Header-retagged codes 2/3/5/6 and
subversion 1 test shared-layout routing, not native-authored ACE generations.
Raw Read/Seek, cursor batches and encoded-image refusal retain original ciphertext;
the tracked original and all upstream fixtures remain unchanged.

The original, synthetic SQL syntax corpus is recorded separately in
[SQL_PARSER.md](../query/SQL_PARSER.md), including its Microsoft grammar references.
It contains no copied third-party fixture bytes or extracted Access SQL and is
not native Access comparison evidence.

object contents: VBA and application object contents have not been decoded or executed.

encryption, schema or object compatibility.

with any redistributed copy; the fixture retains its original attribution rather
than the TableCraft source copyright.

not yet recorded. This third-party application example supplements the original,
recipe-generated minimal fixtures needed for compatibility testing. Qualification
remains incomplete pending those fixtures and oracle evidence. Retain the original bytes and perform

limitation; an initial partial generation is not qualified. This does not establish
GUID physical-index ordering, native Access, cascades or write compatibility.

## Original ACE 16 attachment payload fixtures

The explicit decoded query view now passes 162 stored-record/logical-decoder
controls in `testing/compare_attachment_queries.py` across the original V2007,
V2016 and V2019 inputs. The same public Jackcess getters supply exact original
bytes beside aligned metadata; the sources retain the hashes below and in the
[ACE 2007 record](#ace-2007-stored-attachment-and-history-inputs). Disposable checksum-corrupt
copies prove that late data failures publish no rows under TOP/filters, while
metadata remains readable, including unprojected payload predicates beside
displayed metadata. Unprojected metadata predicates now retain the same child
without adding the criterion member to output. This is an explicit TableCraft
decoded view, not native Access SQL FileData or Windows evidence. No new third-party bytes are
tracked; the existing recipe/artifact provenance applies.
Joined scalar-parent metadata/data predicates now retain parent-pair cardinality;
a late bad payload still fails under joined TOP criteria. The separate multivalue
parent recipe passes 225 controls (including the previous 192) over the already
registered V2007/V2016/V2019 inputs, with unchanged hashes. These comparisons
use explicit nullable membership/absent-parent models, not native Access SQL.

Mixed queries now retain displayed children while independently folding hidden
members from another collection or alias. The parent recipe adds 21 controls for
shown Ints/hidden Texts, negative folds, derived/grouped queries, alias membership
using displayed items and eager non-finite errors. The attachment recipe adds 12
controls for displayed metadata beside hidden alias metadata/decoded data and
buffered late checksum errors. They reuse all original getters, reviewed JAR and
input hashes; no new fixture bytes are tracked. These remain explicit TableCraft
models, not Microsoft Access SQL qualification.

Collection crosstabs add 30 parent-model and 15 attachment controls on these same
original inputs. Explicit Python grids retain all pre-WHERE headings while
membership controls cell rows; tests cover fixed order, empty matches, pivot-only
flattening, mixed/joined criteria, decoded counts and buffered non-finite/checksum
errors. Getter records and hashes remain unchanged; no new artifact is added.
These do not establish native Access crosstab/collection or evaluation-order rules.

Membership subqueries add 30 parent and 12 attachment controls using the same
original getters, hashes and reviewed JAR. Explicit nullable models cover scalar/
IN/NOT IN criteria, multilevel item correlation, nested local collections, joined/
mixed/crosstab reads, eager division errors and opt-in decoded metadata/data NULL
predicates. Empty-list membership remains distinct from an actual NULL or
non-NULL item inside EXISTS. Corrupt payload controls use disposable copies and
publish no stdout. No SQL oracle, native Access evidence or new artifact is added.

Unprojected ON members add 33 parent/child and 18 attachment controls on the same
inert inputs. Python independently expands stored children and models matching,
LEFT/RIGHT preserved rows, duplicates, aligned WHERE siblings, independent parent
membership, grouped/crosstab counts and eager non-finite/decoder errors. ON-only
scalar values and opted-in decoded NULL tests use existing getters; all original
hashes stay unchanged. These models do not qualify Microsoft Access collection
ON admissibility, duplicate/empty behavior or native error ordering.

Several-hidden-member atoms add 33 parent/child and 21 attachment controls using
these same original records, hashes and reviewed JAR. Python independently folds
nullable pair products and zipped stored file records, including metadata/data
NULL criteria, nested capture, mixed/crosstab results and late failures. The
100-item full deeply correlated product is an explicit default row-work refusal;
a bounded scalar ON domain separately qualifies the same captured scopes.
Budgets stay unchanged. These remain independent models, not native Access SQL,
NULL/negative/absent, binary or optimizer/error-order qualification.

literal cell closures. Fourteen candidates (two exact no-ops), fourteen
reverse-order controls, and sixteen invalid requests were observed. Every table,
including untouched components, agrees with the independent complete-row reader;
long values and all native index cursors agree. Discovered closures are byte-equal
to explicit full batches. RESTRICT key swaps, conflicting/pinned requests, stale
expectations and repeated cells reject with typed errors, no stdout and no output
file. Existing outputs and original bytes are preserved. Candidate manifests hash
newline-joined candidate SHA-256 strings in runner order, without a final newline;
reverse controls and explicit reference batches are not counted twice.

stored values across 32 collections, 100-item and 101-item controls, exact IEEE/
GUID/Decimal, Unicode, NULLs and duplicates. The complex file has three parent rows
with Text choices, encoded attachments and raw stored versions; its nine scoped
key/count pairs match independent public APIs. Raw encoded storage is distinct
from typed attachment/history interpretation or content execution.

The existing runners accept optional ACE 2007 inputs while retaining the original
ACE 16 cases. `compare_multivalues.py --ace2007 ... --complex2007 ...` matches all
32 collections/137 exact values and twelve orphan/hint corruption rejections.
Its canonical collection hash remains
`a509aee1a4e181767cff52473c80ed1f569800f33f310ead6007e1c4bafec9de`.
`compare_complex_links.py --ace2007 ...` matches nine memberships and twelve
atomic corrupt-copy refusals, with canonical raw-link hash
`7ed873ac68964513734c4006689fe5828dc89cbabfbe13c325f9fe24c941ff92`.
`compare_multivalue_pages.py --matrix2007 ...` adds eleven page/overflow/refusal/
corruption controls, bringing the unchanged original matrix's total to 57.
NaN payloads remain exact in native values; CLI text only identifies NaN.

These results support the bounded ACE 2007 key/multivalue profile and preserve
Large Number, cached-result, typed attachment/history and write gates. They do
not establish native Access collection semantics, lookup evaluation, `.Value`
query expansion, application round trips or concurrent behavior.

Generate on fresh paths with the reviewed artifact, then pass these files and
the existing ACE 16 inputs to the runners above:

```sh
java -cp "$JACKCESS_JAR" docs/testing/MultivalueFixture.java "$FRESH_VALUES_PATH" V2007
java -cp "$JACKCESS_JAR" docs/testing/ComplexRowsFixture.java "$FRESH_COMPLEX_PATH" V2007
```

coercion, alternate-calendar and Extended Date/Time qualification remains pending.

## Explicit floating conversion read regression

Existing original V2000/V2007/V2016/V2019 scalar files now pass 245 controls per

and buffered errors. All original hashes remain unchanged; no new binary fixture,

## Checked Decimal arithmetic read regression

The existing original ScalarFixture Jet 4/V2000 and ACE V2007/V2016/V2019 inputs
passed 275 controls each (1,100 total), with all original hashes unchanged.
Five added controls per family check native Amount +1/-1/*2//2 and Sum/Avg, retaining
NULL fields and exact independent Fraction results; these chosen aggregate/division
results are exactly representable. The original modern ACE fixtures additionally
check CDec(Large Number)+1 beyond signed-i64 endpoints and exact Decimal summation
of extrema/beyond-Double integers, unchanged. Explicit CDec NULL failures are
retained: the aggregate conversion comparison filters NULL before calling CDec.

No new binary inputs were introduced. Existing Java recipes, reviewed JAR and
provenance remain unchanged; compare_browsing.py and compare_ace16_scalars.py own
the new controls. Kernel properties separately compare bounded operations with
independent i128/exact rational half-even models, including recurring division.
These are not observed native Access SQL result-type, rounding, locale or
aggregate comparisons; those remain pending.

semijoin model in Python. Forty-six controls cover sequential/Number-index input,
hidden-field multiplicity, ordinary DISTINCT, TOP, all-source/single-source ignored
cases, filters and complete/partial/computed, UNION and grouped derived records.
Correlated/nested/shadowed subquery projections, empty scalar results and all-source
captures also have independent visible-multiplicity controls. Field-free literals,
closed subqueries, global/grouped counts and grouped constant outputs preserve
ordinary joined-input multiplicities in ten additional controls. Source bytes must
remain unchanged. Library controls additionally exercise named
and fixed/discovered crosstab sources, explicit Error-state refusals and inherited
text-policy routing. Authored synthetic Jet 4/ACE 2007/ACE 16-family adapter inputs
reuse the original mutation/Caption recipe without adding a database binary.
Subquery-projection fixtures also check one/both-source capture ownership through
two query frames. Library controls count per-row clock/random callbacks and retain
cardinality/late failures, Error data and every observed cancellation checkpoint. This compares stored rows
and an independent relational model, not native Access DISTINCTROW execution.
No new database or third-party artifact is tracked.

not qualified by this ordinary schedule model. Windows Access precision, coercion,
edge behavior and save/reopen remain unrun. Hashes identify the observed files,
not deterministic generation promises.

cases additionally validate cashflow residuals from returned rates/payments.

explicitly. Cases include zero/negative/positive rates, supplied guesses selecting
two roots, explicit native parameters, grouped/derived queries, UNION, crosstabs,
iteration exhaustion, impossible targets and buffered late errors. Jackcess only
reads/writes inert metadata and renders SQL. All original bytes remain unchanged.

[The comparison runner](compare_hyperlink.py) passes 1090 independent
component/refusal/native-field controls, including typed raw getters and all six
component projections with unchanged source hashes. Recorded hashes identify
this original generation; repeat generation can contain different metadata bytes.
Generation refuses existing outputs. The initial unqualified harness run used
an incorrect null-tag decoder; its partial output is not included in this evidence.
The corrected run uses four fresh files. Jackcess constructs/decodes data without
executing HyperlinkPart; actual Microsoft Access evaluation/save remains unrun.

The same original Hyperlink files also pass 628 independent UTF-16-byte/refusal/
native-field controls through [the byte observation runner](compare_byte_strings.py).
LenB covers every row, including empty/NULL Text; AscB runs only on nonempty Text.
Expected lengths/first bytes come from Python UTF-16LE encoding of independent
Jackcess-decoded fields. All four original file hashes remain unchanged; native
Microsoft Access evaluation and compressed field-size equivalence are not claimed.

The extended byte-string runner now passes 3208 controls with those same four
original inputs. In addition to LenB/AscB, it checks ChrB across all byte codes
and LeftB/RightB/MidB against Python byte slicing, including odd lengths and
unaligned UTF-16 boundaries. Native field controls compare first-three-byte
lengths and last-byte codes from independent getters. All original hashes remain
unchanged; Jackcess still supplies data rather than evaluating the functions.

Binary InStrB extends that same runner to 3952 controls. Python byte searches
cover all 256 single-byte needles, Unicode patterns, empty inputs and varied byte
starts. Native queries search each original Hyperlink value for a zero byte,
including NULL/empty rows, against the independent getter-derived UTF-16LE
model. File hashes remain unchanged. This is independent reader/model evidence,
not Microsoft Access function execution or linguistic byte-search qualification.

[The StrConv runner](compare_strconv.py) passes 12178 independent controls.
It exhausts byte decoding and exact encode-back behavior for the eleven existing
single-byte pages using Python codecs plus the reviewed WHATWG exceptions, and
checks selected casing models and refusal cases in both query modes. Native
controls compare upper/lower/proper casing over all original Hyperlink rows,
ANSI round trips/lengths over their representable subset, and refusal of an
unmappable row. Every original file hash remains unchanged. This establishes
independent model/reader evidence, not native Windows StrConv execution or NLS
mapping, best-fit, word-start or error-order equivalence.

[The numeric Format runner](compare_format.py) passes 2570 independent
Decimal/model, section/literal/refusal and native-field controls. Original native
Int32 IDs exercise zero-padding, negative sections and exact eighth fractions in
percent output; decoded NULL rows exercise explicit fourth-section literals.
All four original hashes remain unchanged. Python supplies the declared numeric
model and Jackcess supplies stored values; neither establishes actual Access
Format/NLS/error semantics or the unfinished date/text mask families.

checks multiplication's Double serial result instead of refusal and passes its
62 model controls. These are independent models/getters, not native Access typing,
optimizer, error-order or save/reopen qualification.

and native Byte/Integer/Single/DATE projections. The four original ScalarFixture
inputs from the Format date-mask corpus above retain their hashes. Reviewed
Jackcess 5.0.4 supplies read-only getters with expression evaluation disabled;
the reference JAR is hash checked and original input bytes remain unchanged.
No third-party implementation or binary is copied. The existing Text arithmetic
runner also passes its 672 controls. Only TableCraft executes SQL, so native
Access typing, rounding, optimizer and error-order qualification remain open.

Original Rust/Python ZIP fixtures include empty directory markers beside stored

all temporary package bytes remain unchanged by the reader. No third-party
fixture or native Access result is introduced.

The original MIT [LargeIndexFixture.java](LargeIndexFixture.java) creates

and buffered failures. Only TableCraft evaluates SQL. No third-party fixture,
code, native Access execution or additional format qualification is supplied.

## Persistent large-index corpus restoration (23 October 2026)

The previously temporary Jackcess JAR and four large-index inputs were missing
from the local filesystem. The reviewed JAR was fetched again from
[Maven Central](https://repo.maven.apache.org/maven2/com/healthmarketscience/jackcess/jackcess/5.0.4/jackcess-5.0.4.jar)
and matched the existing SHA-256
`19a7494a56f439a57fda85c059b019b665802085cec5ae8186d1e028cc1bdfbc`.
The original MIT LargeIndexFixture recipe generated fresh files; this restores
the authored dataset, not the exact lost bytes. Catalog metadata timestamps can
change. The earlier identities and historical evidence above remain intact.

Artifacts now live under ignored `.cache/tablecraft-fixtures/`, including the
JAR, `large-indexes/`, a recipe/JAR/file SHA-256 inventory and raw logs. These
files stay outside Git and ordinary Cargo cleanup. No downloaded fixture or
binary is published. Jackcess remains an Apache-2.0 engineering tool, not a
TableCraft runtime dependency or Microsoft Access oracle.

| Restored generated input | Bytes | SHA-256 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `index-jet4.mdb` | 8,355,840 | `4ca8dcdf0f66f3e6688db19573b1846c326f9c7416ad8f6caf3a687675f0018f` |
| `index-2007.accdb` | 8,585,216 | `7605dae8ff18fb13ee5a39f9e3f53ea97e65d829ec5642095a488787cdf491c3` |
| `index-2016.accdb` | 9,003,008 | `339d125b40367eaa11abd4ceff26ea69f5aab62bc9a82d76ba05436b274fbefe` |
| `index-2019.accdb` | 9,072,640 | `75a6a875a62a3a4e7362eaf47383541b47ef9198a9daa2fd2eae8ac530c123c4` |

Large-index consumers accept only the separately registered original/restored
identities for the selected profile. Each run captures the chosen exact digest;
existing after-read comparisons still require that digest, rather than accepting
a different generation midway through the run. Unknown bytes and profile swaps
fail. This is not a flag to disable fixture verification or trust arbitrary local
metadata. A new regeneration requires review and registration of its identities.

Generation and the existing 288 independent index/full/projection/seek/cache/
refusal controls passed. All four restored identities matched, a different-profile
selection failed, a one-byte mutation on a disposable copy failed, and original
hashes remained unchanged. No native Access comparison or save/reopen was run.

cursors supply IDs, Python supplies string ordering, and all database bytes are
checked unchanged. Selected-index controls exercise Error mode precedence and
explicit CStr/Error formatting. These are independent models and native-source
integration checks, not Microsoft Access SQL comparison evidence.


The initial decoded batch exhausted the cumulative 256 MiB allowance because it
constructed/charged a fresh inflater per file. Explicit budget-bound sessions now
reuse that real workspace, while preserving per-file checks and allocation/work
charges. No budget maximum was raised. The separate [benchmark evidence](ATTACHMENT_SESSIONS.md)
retains matched and excluded runs; correctness relies on the exact controls above.

## Larger buffered collection-page controls

Original [compare_large_collection_pages.py](compare_large_collection_pages.py)

binary fixture, copied content or active database execution is introduced.

Eighty-eight CLI controls pass across ACE 16 and ACE 2007, covering multivalue,
attachment and stored-history pages. They verify explicit larger budgets, offsets,

lower-cap refusals and unchanged inputs. Successful mixed-table `--all` sections

outside the displayed range, attachment/history bindings and every observed
successful cancellation checkpoint. A browser control proves cumulative byte

small-pool refusals and invalid ranges before I/O. Native Access, encrypted large
collection pages, million-row and live-file qualification were not added here.

| wide-V2019.accdb | 503,808 | b560cb9a3c9a6ad4648393f4ab06763f821830b952529b7201e63000b6aec3bc |

Two 255-column/18-row tables per profile retain nine schema-width generations,
updated/deleted/added rows, wide variable tails and mixed fixed/Boolean/Text
storage. The [contract](WIDE_TABLE_HISTORIES.md) records 112 independent
model/getter/native/CLI/query controls, unchanged originals and two portable
Rust framing tests. Databases, reviewed JAR, inventory and logs remain ignored
under .cache/tablecraft-fixtures/. Native Access execution and save/reopen are
unrun; engineering generation does not establish them.


### Maximum-width corruption derivatives

The original MIT WideRowLocationOracle.java opens registered wide-table inputs
read-only with expressions disabled and reports root locations. The original
compare_wide_row_failures.py runner verifies each registered input hash, creates
only fresh temporary derivatives, and changes five framing words in the last
appended row of each table. It checks 280 buffered/query/streaming outcomes and
both original/derivative immutability during reads. No derivative is retained as
a binary fixture or added to CI. The original generation hashes, reviewed JAR
and license boundary above remain unchanged; native Access is unrun. See
[the failure contract](../access/storage/NATIVE_READ_FAILURES.md#maximum-width-malformed-suffixes).


### QBColor Integer boundary controls

The original compare_colors.py model now distinguishes Integer overflow from
palette Domain refusal using independent Python Decimal half-even/range checks.
Scalar eval/run cover integral, fractional, numeric Text and CDec arguments at
both Int16 endpoints, alongside the existing packed-channel/palette controls.
The 23 October 2026 run passes 354 scalar controls; no optional native database
was supplied. No new fixture bytes, external code, tools or dependencies are
introduced; native Access query-wrapper/error qualification remains unrun.


### Choose Single-index model controls

The original compare_conditional_functions.py runner now packs indexes as IEEE
binary32 before independent Python Decimal half-even rounding. Near-half cases
cover literal, Decimal (within its own envelope), numeric Text and Single
sources, along with overflow/eager-error controls. The reviewed Jackcess JAR hash
is verified before reference execution. The run passes 140 controls: 136 scalar
and four sequential/selected-index native-row queries over the registered MIT
Access example. Its SHA-256 remains
3cd1fc88d70bc93909ae6d190fa9692e709d5641b205fd2f66185c905e42cfbf.
Only TableCraft evaluates SQL. No new database/tool bytes are introduced or
tracked; native Access query-wrapper precision and error qualification are unrun.


### Query Nz metadata and UNION controls

Original MIT compare_nz_metadata.py reuses the registered maximum-width inputs
and reviewed Jackcess 5.0.4 JAR, verifying hashes before reference execution.
Independent Python recipe values are checked against inert getters. Across the
four priority layouts, 112 controls cover omitted Text fallbacks, explicit numeric
fallbacks, both UNION arm orders, sequential/selected-index reads, required numeric
rendering policy and late/eager buffered refusals. Original hashes remain unchanged.
No new binary fixtures or tools are introduced; only TableCraft evaluates SQL.
Native Access result typing, query-wrapper/coercion and error equivalence are unrun.

### Same-carrier selector constraint controls

The original compare_nz_metadata.py runner now also covers explicit Nz fallbacks
and IIf/Switch/Choose selections between native bounded Text and unconstrained
literal Text. Existing registered maximum-width inputs, reviewed JAR and raw
getters remain unchanged. The expanded 240-control run checks sequential/indexed
UNION rendering, explicit-policy requirements and buffered failures across Jet 4,
ACE 2007 and both ACE 16 layouts. Only TableCraft executes SQL; this is independent
model/getter evidence, with native Access result typing/promotion unrun.


## Integral inputs in floating-selected sums

The extended original MIT `testing/compare_floating_sum.py` runner now passes
519 controls using the same reviewed Jackcess 5.0.4 tool and registered
20,000-row input identities. The additional 176 scalar controls model LongLong
conversion from exact Python Fraction values through nearest binary64 rounding;
twelve native controls use inert BigValue and Single/Double getters on the two
modern ACE inputs. Sum/DSum execute in TableCraft through sequential and both
ID-index orders. The existing four-profile original hashes and bytes remain
unchanged. No binary fixture, third-party source or native Access evidence is
added. See [numerical scope](FLOATING_SUMS.md#integralfloating-integration).


## Statistical numeric admission and explicit DATE serials

The original MIT [compare_statistical_admission.py](compare_statistical_admission.py)
adds no binary input or third-party source. It checks the existing reviewed
Jackcess JAR and four registered large-index corpus identities, reads inert raw
DATE bits, and compares 208 CLI controls. Python Fraction derives sample/population
statistics over explicit serial conversion. Raw Date/Text/Error refusal and NULL
controls remain separate from numeric results. Sequential and ascending/descending
ID indexes cover direct and domain statistics; original bytes/hashes remain
unchanged. Only TableCraft executes SQL; native Access admission, error ordering
and result typing remain unqualified.


## Legacy DATE average controls

The original MIT [compare_date_average.py](compare_date_average.py) adds
no binary fixture or third-party source. It verifies the reviewed Jackcess JAR

Its 144 controls combine 72 source-free Fraction models and 72 native query
cases over Jet 4, ACE 2007 and both modern ACE layouts. Sequential and both
ID-index directions exercise Avg/DAvg, derived/grouped/crosstab means, empty
inputs and mixed serial/numeric expressions. Source hashes and bytes remain
unchanged. Only TableCraft executes SQL; no native Access result-carrier,

## Ordinary Date/numeric comparison controls

The original MIT [compare_date_numeric_predicates.py](compare_date_numeric_predicates.py)
adds no binary fixture or third-party source. It checks the reviewed Jackcess JAR
and existing registered four-family large-index inputs, derives calendar order

nine numeric conversion families, range/NULL/eager-error boundaries, filters,
membership/ranges and domain criteria through sequential and both ID-index orders.
Original hashes and bytes remain unchanged. Only TableCraft executes SQL; native

## Attachment presence replay

The original `testing/compare_attachment_presence.py` reuses the registered
`AttachmentMetadataFixture.java` corpus and Jackcess 5.0.4. It adds no binary

results use independently derived parent identities and membership counts. Each registered original hash is checked
before and after execution. These are independent source/model controls, not

```sh
python3 docs/testing/compare_attachment_presence.py --cli target/debug/tablecraft --jar path/to/jackcess-5.0.4.jar --directory path/to/attachment-metadata
```

output caps publish nothing. Native Access SQL, performance and automatic non-NULL
query selection remain unqualified/unimplemented as described in
[the owning contract](../access/indexes/NATIVE_TEXT.md#conservative-short-text-equality-candidates).

with expression evaluation disabled and a throwing LinkResolver. Sixteen engine
comparisons pass across all four profiles with zero target-resolution calls and
all eight original hashes unchanged.

The [feature contract](../access/schema/NATIVE_LINKED_TABLES.md) and
[accepted report](results/linked-metadata-2026-10-24.json) distinguish inert
catalog metadata from target activation. No credentials, active content or saved
queries execute. Native Access execution, automatic provider/path resolution,
linked query semantics and writes remain unqualified. Fixture databases, decoded
oracle output, reference JAR and frozen executable remain ignored local artifacts.

neither reference SQL nor active content executes.

through explicit lower-level MSysObjects updates for provider, ODBC type, Database,
native identity or link-chain refusals. These are controlled metadata cases,
not additional native writer qualifications. Five `lowercase-frontend.*` copies
receive a public createLinkedTable addition whose stored ForeignName is lowercase.
All original pairs stay unchanged. The final reports pin every selected controlled
copy and lowercase addition; database bytes are not tracked.

are not counted as runtime depletion evidence; positive-one runtime controls
are recorded separately. Explicit host paths/handles provide target authority;
stored Database text does not.

## External JDBC aggregate metadata probe

`testing/JdbcAggregateMetadataProbe.java` and
`testing/compare_jdbc_aggregate_metadata.py` are original Apache-2.0 tools. They
reuse the four registered `scalar-seek-20261023` original Scalar fixtures listed
above, copy them into fresh ignored work directories and verify unchanged original
and copy hashes. No new third-party fixture or implementation bytes are tracked.
The [observations](JDBC_AGGREGATE_METADATA.md) record exact external
UCanAccess 5.2.0/Jackcess 5.2.0/HSQLDB 2.7.4 artifact hashes, POM/license sources
and checksum review; their JARs/notices remain in the ignored artifact cache.
The [report](results/jdbc-aggregate-metadata-final-2026-10-25.json) retains
232 independent reference queries (including eight reference errors) and 232
successful TableCraft queries. This is JDBC metadata/value evidence, without
native Access qualification or a changed TableCraft numeric profile.

## Page-buffered encryption copy controls

The page-copy tests reuse the previously recorded `Access_Example_VBA.accdb`,
original deterministic Agile support and reviewed legacy vectors. Header-retagged
Jet/ACE derivatives exercise existing codec routing and do not establish native
file creation or compiled-application compatibility. Originals remain unchanged.

The CLI scale test writes a temporary copy of that example and extends it with
unallocated zero pages to exactly 65 MiB. It adds encryption, changes the password,
removes encryption, compares every decoded page (allowing the documented ACE 2007
header promotion), compares all data-page ciphertext across the Agile password
change, and queries the existing 38-row table. The derived files are removed with
the test's temporary directory. No additional database bytes or external fixture
license are introduced. This proves the larger page-copy path, not a 65 MiB native
application or the complete 2 GiB envelope. See the
[verification record](results/encryption-page-copies-2026-10-25.json).

Each original has Indexed, CodecKeys, FloatingKeys, Unindexed and Sentinel
tables. Indexed combines primary, unique/nonunique ascending/descending indexes,
a reversed composite and the actual GroupAsc/GroupTwin physical alias. Its rows
include Unicode Memo and 8 KiB OLE payloads. CodecKeys covers Boolean, Byte,
Int16, Int32, Int64, Currency, GUID and Decimal indexes plus a mixed tuple.
FloatingKeys covers finite Single/Double signed zero and ties, NULL, and legacy
DATE indexes. No index ignores NULLs; there are no relationships. Every physical
index owns one page in the getter observations. Reference operations delete ID2,
nullable-key ID3, or all rows from the four nonsentinel tables; Sentinel remains
untouched.

The [reference capture](results/delete-candidate-reference-2026-10-26.json)
contains eight read-only getter commands and raw output. Eleven file pins
(producer, capture script, JAR and eight databases) stay unchanged across capture.
Exact table/logical-index inventories remain present after delete-all; complete
surviving row/index populations and surviving scalar/Memo/OLE getter values are
checked. Locations, physical index identities, historical `uniqueEntryCount`,
encounter order and exact IEEE/date getter bits are recorded. Pinned implementation
metadata getters supplement public row/index cursors; they are observations of
this reviewed dependency version, not format authority.

This chunk does not run a TableCraft DELETE candidate or qualify ordinary writes,
data macros, node contraction, durable apply or native Access save/reopen. Fresh
generation is not claimed byte-reproducible. The first smaller corpus and
superseded getter captures remain ignored and unchanged. Getter-only output
additions after generation did not regenerate originals. The final capture uses
relative provenance paths; its superseded predecessor and exact runner snapshot
retain the original machine-path labels privately.

From the repository root, create a fresh existing directory, then run:

```sh
for profile in V2016 V2019; do
  java -Dstdout.encoding=UTF-8 -cp .cache/tablecraft-fixtures/jackcess-5.0.4.jar \
    docs/testing/DeleteCandidateFixture.java generate FRESH_DIRECTORY "$profile"
done
python3 docs/testing/capture_delete_candidates.py \
  --jar .cache/tablecraft-fixtures/jackcess-5.0.4.jar \
  --inputs FRESH_DIRECTORY --report FRESH_REPORT.json
```

Selected output filenames and the report must not already exist. The capture
uses a 120-second timeout per Java getter command.

map-payload/bitmap-page checks show only the exact retirement bits changing;
representations/pointers remain unchanged. Original record bodies, Binary
